```text
       European countries achieved NATIONAL CONSOLIDATION BEFORE
       modernising -> precedence to STATE-BUILDING and NATION-BUILDING
     >> this is the axis that answers the question's SECOND half,
        because it denies that the two can be RANKED at all --
        they must be SEQUENCED, and the sequence runs the other way

  STEP 3  CLOSE ON A GRADED, CONDITIONAL VERDICT
     never a flat yes or no; name the CONDITIONS under which democracy
     promotes development, and the FAILURE MODE under which it does not
```

### 1. Why the question must be re-framed before it is answered

As posed, the question assumes development is a single measurable thing against which political systems can be scored. Lesson 10 denied that: development is measured on an **empirical** scale and **progress** applies a **normative** criterion, so a system can raise the first while failing the second. Lesson 15 added that the concept was built for a purpose and that its three printed definitions emphasise **capacity** (Mittelman), **structure** (Baran) and the **person** (Rodney) rather than output.

🔑 The decisive consequence is this: once development includes **increasing capacity**, **greater freedom, creativity, self-discipline and responsibility**, democracy is **partly constitutive** of development rather than merely instrumental to it. The question then stops being a contest between two goods and becomes a question about which features of democratic practice serve, and which obstruct, a development so defined. ⚠️ That move is the single highest-value thing in this lesson, and it costs two sentences.

### 2. The four axes, with the counter-pressure on each

**Axis 1 — participation and responsiveness.** Democracy **promotes** development where participation is justified **developmentally**, since it **enhances moral, social and political awareness**, and where **responsive** capability — one of the four in Lesson 21 — is treated as a measure of political development subject to the **balance rule**. It **hinders** where the printed crowd objection bites: benefits **come very late**, and those with the **skill to gather and mobilize crowds** secure **unreasonable demands** while modest petitioners **go unheard**.

**Axis 2 — substantive against procedural.** The Marxist case is that procedure without transformed production is **an eyewash**, that suffrage was conceded only once property relations were safe, and that periodic choice means deciding which representatives will **represent and repress**. The counter is the substantive route's own record: an arrangement that **may be more conducive to social justice, but is by no means an example of democracy**, and a commune that failed **primarily due to the abolition of incentives for better work**. ⚠️ Neither side wins outright, and saying so is the correct result rather than a failure of nerve.

**Axis 3 — the path chosen.** The same question receives four answers because the models use different criteria. The **market** model treats growth as necessary and values democracy where it secures opportunity and merit, while conceding that welfare is gained **at the expense of their moral strength**. The **welfare** model makes democracy the source of the standing that converts relief into **mutual assistance and self-reliance**, with a ceiling of **capacity** — built **only on a subdued scale**. The **socialist** model delivers employment, education and health care **although they hardly care to protect civil liberties and political rights**. The **Gandhian** model changes the criterion altogether, so a democracy maximising want-satisfaction may be developing and not progressing. ⚠️ Across all four runs the ecological limit: three are recorded as **Indifferent** to nature, and growth that consumes **the very basis on which it has been erected** is mis-measured at source.

**Axis 4 — sequencing.** The modernise-first thesis holds that GNP, urbanisation, rational bureaucracy, manipulative technology and social mobilisation come first and political development follows. **Kothari (1976)** refutes it on the evidence: **European history does not corroborate it**, because European countries achieved **national consolidation before embarking on modernisation**, so **state-building and nation-building take precedence**. 🔑 This axis answers the question's second half by denying that the two can be ranked at all — they are sequenced, and the sequence runs the other way from the one the stem assumes.

### 3. The verdict

A graded verdict states conditions and failure modes rather than a preference. Democracy **promotes** development where participation is channelled rather than merely permitted, where responsive and distributive capability match regulative and extractive capability, where procedure is accompanied by attention to material conditions, and where state-building and nation-building are not deferred in favour of output. It **hinders** development where mobilisation capacity substitutes for reasoned claim, where procedural form is treated as sufficient, where a single development path is adopted as if it were the only criterion, and where the ecological limit is excluded from measurement. ⚠️ On the printed definitions the question is therefore not whether democracy promotes development but **which features of democratic practice are constitutive of it and which are corrosive of it** — and that reformulation is the answer.

### Deployment in GS-II, GS-IV and the Essay

- **GS-II.** This is the structure for any stem pairing democracy with delivery, growth, capacity or reform; the four axes convert it into four paragraphs with a built-in counter-pressure in each.
- **GS-IV.** The constitutive move — that agency is part of development rather than a means to it — is the cleanest statement of why a beneficiary is not the same as a citizen.
- **Essay.** The reformulation in the verdict is a complete essay thesis: the real question is which features of democratic practice build development and which erode it.

**Answer framework — the assembled answer, in five moves.** Refuse the framing and fix the conception, naming the empirical/normative split → make the constitutive move, which reorganises the whole answer → run two or three axes with the counter-pressure attached to each, not four, in a fifteen-marker → bring in the sequencing refutation, which is the strongest closing available → end on conditions and failure modes. ⚠️ ❌ No country is named as an instance at any point, and the answer is stronger for it, because the argument rests on criteria rather than on cases.

Revision notes:

- ⚠️ The question has **no single printed home**; it is assembled across six page clusters. Step 0: refuse the framing — fix **development** before scoring any political system.
- Step 1: **empirical** development against **normative** progress; Mittelman's **capacity**, Baran's **structure**, Rodney's **person**.
- 🔑 The constitutive move: on these definitions democracy is **partly constitutive** of development, not merely instrumental to it.
- Axis 1 promotes: the **developmental** justification of participation; **responsive capability** and the **balance rule**.
- Axis 1 hinders: benefits **come very late**; crowd-mobilisers secure **unreasonable demands**; modest petitioners **go unheard**.
- Axis 2 hinders: procedure without transformed production is **an eyewash**; **represent and repress**.
- Axis 2 counter: **more conducive to social justice, but by no means an example of democracy**; the commune failed through **the abolition of incentives for better work**.
- Axis 3: four paths, four criteria — market's **moral strength** trade-off; welfare's **subdued scale** ceiling; socialist delivery **although they hardly care to protect civil liberties**; the Gandhian change of criterion.
- Axis 3 limit: three models **Indifferent** to nature; growth consuming **the very basis on which it has been erected**.
- Axis 4: **Kothari (1976)** — European **national consolidation before modernising** → precedence to **state-building and nation-building**.
- Verdict: graded and conditional; ❌ never a flat yes or no, and ❌ no country named as an instance.
- 🔑 The reformulation that is the answer: which features of democratic practice are **constitutive** of development, and which are **corrosive** of it.

### Concept check

**Question:** Build the answer to "does democracy promote or hinder development?" in the order the evidence requires, and state the verdict it supports.

**Model answer:** The question cannot be scored until development is fixed, so the first move is definitional: development is **stronger, more organized, more efficient, more effective** and **more satisfying in terms of human wants and aspirations**, assessed on an **empirical** scale, while **progress** applies a **normative** criterion; and the three printed definitions emphasise **capacity** (Mittelman), **structure** (Baran) and the **person** (Rodney), including **greater freedom, creativity, self-discipline and responsibility**. 🔑 On those definitions democracy is **partly constitutive** of development rather than instrumental to it, which reorganises the question. Four axes then test it. **Participation and responsiveness:** participation is justified **developmentally**, since it **enhances moral, social and political awareness**, and **responsive capability** is itself a measure of political development under the **balance rule** — but benefits **come very late** and those with the **skill to gather and mobilize crowds** secure **unreasonable demands** while modest petitioners **go unheard**. **Substantive against procedural:** procedure without transformed production is **an eyewash**, and suffrage was conceded once property relations were safe — yet the substantive route produced an arrangement **more conducive to social justice** that is **by no means an example of democracy**, and a commune that failed through **the abolition of incentives for better work**. **The path chosen:** market, welfare, socialist and Gandhian models answer differently because their criteria differ, and across all four runs the ecological limit, three being recorded as **Indifferent** to nature. **Sequencing: Kothari (1976)** refutes the modernise-first thesis because **European history does not corroborate it** — consolidation preceded modernisation — so **state-building and nation-building take precedence**. ⚠️ The verdict is graded: democracy promotes development where participation is channelled, where responsive and distributive capability match regulative and extractive capability, where procedure is accompanied by attention to material conditions, and where state-building is not deferred; it hinders development where mobilisation substitutes for reasoned claim, where procedure is treated as sufficient, where one path is adopted as the only criterion, or where ecological cost is excluded from measurement. ❌ No country is named as an instance.

**Misconception to avoid:** Do not answer this question by comparing political systems on growth outcomes. The topic supplies no such evidence, the printed definitions are not output measures, and the comparison would require classifying present-day states, which is prohibited here. The examinable content is the criteria and the sequencing argument, not a verdict about particular regimes — and an answer built on criteria is both safer and stronger than one built on contested cases.

**Original Mains practice [10 marks]:** "Democracy is a means to development." Comment. *(Comment — answer in about 150 words.)*

**Model (within 150 words):** The proposition treats democracy as instrumental, and the printed definitions resist that. **Where it holds.** If development is measured as output, democratic procedure is one institutional arrangement among others, assessed by what it delivers — the market model's position, and the premise of the modernise-first thesis. **Where it fails.** Development is defined as **increasing capacity to make rational use of natural and human resources for social ends** and as **a many-sided process** implying greater freedom, creativity, **self-discipline** and responsibility; participation is justified **developmentally**, because it **enhances moral, social and political awareness**; and **responsive** capability is itself a criterion of political development. On those definitions democratic agency is part of what development **is**. ⚠️ **Kothari (1976)** completes the refusal: European countries achieved **national consolidation before modernising**, so political construction precedes rather than serves economic transformation. Verdict: instrumental in part, constitutive in part — and the proposition omits the constitutive part.

**Rubric (quantified, 10 marks: 3 + 3 + 2 + 2):** 3 for conceding the instrumental reading properly, locating it in an output conception and in the modernise-first thesis rather than dismissing it; 3 for the constitutive case built from the printed definitions — capacity, freedom, responsibility — plus the developmental justification of participation, since an answer asserting that democracy is intrinsically valuable without these has changed the subject; 2 for the sequencing refutation with its European premise; 2 for a two-part verdict naming what the proposition omits. Deduct 2 for comparing regimes on growth outcomes, and 2 for naming any country as an instance of democratic or authoritarian development.

---

**End of the teaching arc — and of the subject.** Topic 23 is the last of the twenty-three Political-Theory topics and the last chapter block of the source: the printed text ends at PDF p.575 (printed 556) on Kothari's sequencing argument, after which p.576 begins *Further Reading*. ❌ There is no next topic and no forward pointer. What follows is practice, consolidation and the ledger.

# VERIFIED PYQ LINKAGE AND ANSWER APPROACHES

## Ownership

Political Theory is **supplementary conceptual support for General Studies**. The subject's own mapping records that this folder is **not a direct named General Studies owner**, that the count of central question routes into it is **zero by design**, and that no proxy, alias, mirror ledger or synthetic assignment may be created to produce a non-zero count. ⚠️ That zero follows from the conceptual-support boundary; it is not a coverage gap. For this topic the mapping names `basic/23` in exactly **one** clause row — the **Essay** clause, as part of a concept bank, status **PARTIAL**, with direct Essay ownership delegated to `Essay/`. The mapping's own boundary note records that the post-source modules **deepen the conceptual slice only**, do not upgrade any clause status, and delegate their outward remainders.

**Nothing in this edition is presented as a Political Theory previous-year question, because none exists.** Every stem below is a **deployment target owned by another file**, named at each use, and no answer approach is offered on its owner's behalf. Every solved item in the next two sections is original practice written for this edition.

📝 Stems follow the frozen source audit's abbreviated rendering of the controlling local ledgers, with typographic quotation marks rendered as straight ASCII. ⚠️ Five are shortened against the ledger wording — 2022 Q3(a), 2024 Q3(c), 2023 Q2(c), 2025 Q3(c) and 2019 Q2(c) drop a trailing directive, an opening clause or an article, and 2024 Q3(c) reads "for social progress" where the ledger reads "in order to achieve social progress". The 2026 stems are verbatim. ❌ Cite the ledger files, not these renderings, when the exact wording matters.

## Primary-demand targets — this topic supplies the concept spine

⚠️ These nine drive the lesson weighting. ❌ They are not claimed, and an Optional candidate should use the named owners rather than this edition.

**2026, Q1(d) [10] — owned by `Political-Ideologies.md` §2.10.**
*"On what grounds does Marxism criticize pure democracy?"*
- **Why it is decisive.** 🔑 The directive is **grounds**, which is a demand for enumeration. The route is **Lesson 4** entire: the *Manifesto* premise; **Plamenatz's four reasons** — schooling bought by wealth, power passing to leaders in large organisations, leaders of modest origin acquiring privileged attitudes, and the wealthy obtaining and controlling **information**; then the **pivot**, that if those were the only grounds, equalising inside capitalism would make the liberal model true; then the three printed mechanisms with **Maguire's** illusory representation, **false consent**, and **Lenin's** periodicity line.
- **What this topic does not supply.** The ideology's full doctrine and its internal schools. ⚠️ Routed to the owner.

**2026, Q4(c) [15] — owned by `Development-Social-Progress.md` §2.7.**
*"Examine the relationship between democracy and development. Does democracy promote or hinder development?"*
- **Why it is decisive.** ⚠️ It has **no single printed section** and must be assembled, which is why **Lesson 23** exists. The route is: fix the conception (L10, L15) → make the constitutive move → test on participation and responsiveness (L3, L21), substantive against procedural (L4, L5), the path chosen (L17–L20), and sequencing (L21) → graded verdict. ❌ No country may be named as an instance.

**2023, Q3(a) [20] — owned by `Political-Ideologies.md`.**
*"Explain Historical Materialism and discuss its relevance in the context of social development and change."*
- **Why it is decisive.** The twenty marks split across the two halves. *Explain* is **Lesson 12** with **Lesson 11** as its printed premise — the three laws are the only printed mechanism and are the commonest omission. *Relevance* is **Lesson 13** plus **Lesson 14**: the midwife and lever formulations, then Popper's falsifiability objection, the two grounds, piecemeal against Utopian engineering, and the two-sided appraisal closing on **Sorel** and **Kafka**.

**2022, Q3(a) [20] — owned by `Development-Social-Progress.md`.**
*"Do you agree that economic development does not on its own lead to human development and social progress?"*
- **Why it is decisive.** The phrase *on its own* is the sufficiency demand of **Lesson 10**, answered analytically from the empirical/normative split. The body is **Lessons 15–19**: the three definitions that refuse the output reduction, the market model's three criticisms, the welfare ceiling, and the Gandhian change of criterion, with the ecological limit from **Lesson 20**.

**2024, Q3(c) [10+5] — owned by `Development-Social-Progress.md`.**
*"Is economic development a necessary condition, sufficient condition, both or neither, for social progress?"*
- **Why it is decisive.** 🔑 It is the clearest test of whether the candidate fixed the conception first. **Sufficiency is refuted analytically** at Lesson 10; **necessity is contested** and is answered from Lesson 17 against Lesson 19. ⚠️ An answer that settles both halves by definition has overreached.

**2020, Q2(a) [20] — owned by `Development-Social-Progress.md`.**
*"State and examine the Gandhian concept of social development."*
- **Why it is decisive.** It is a standalone twenty-marker on one model, so **Lesson 19**'s Gandhian half is the whole answer: *Young India* (1927) and **I call it satanic**; conscience rather than external conditions; **need but not their greed**; **bread labour**; *Harijan* (1935) and the **seven hundred thousand villages**; **production by the masses**; non-violence extended to nature; the balancing line on machinery; and the four-model table's **attitude-toward-nature** row.

**2025, Q3(c) [15] — owned by `Development-Social-Progress.md`.**
*"Is it possible to reconcile development with tribal values to bring social and economic progress?"*
- **Why it matters here.** ⚠️ Only the conceptual half is this topic's: **Lesson 20**'s standing shift from **masters** to **partners**, the **small-scale, self-governing communities** prescription, and **Lesson 19**'s Gandhian criterion. ❌ Tribal-rights statutes and judgments belong to the Philosophy owner and to `Social-Justice/`.

**2019, Q2(c) [15] — owned by `Development-Social-Progress.md`.**
*"Does technological development lead to progress in the ethical standards of society?"*
- **Why it matters here.** The whole question turns on **Lesson 10**'s empirical/normative split, then on **Lesson 17**'s profit objection — people **reduced to self-seeking animals**, welfare gained **at the expense of their moral strength** — and **Lesson 20**'s Schumacher line.

**2023, Q2(c) [15] — owned by `Development-Social-Progress.md`.**
*"Will the emphasis on skill education enhance development?"*
- **Why it matters here.** **Lesson 15**'s capacity-centred definitions plus **Lesson 21**'s **capacity** element and the four capabilities with the **balance rule**. 🔑 The discriminator is the balance rule: skill-building that raises extractive and regulative capacity without responsive and distributive capacity is not political development on this model.
